# Manuscript Front Cover

*This manuscript is bound in vellum—a high-quality parchment made from animal skin—and is decorated with intricate red block-printed designs. Two wide bands of floral and vine-like patterns stretch across the cover, topped by a large, fan-shaped decorative element. The binding shows significant signs of age, including staining and heavy wear, particularly along the spine.*

# Technical Sketches of Pyrotechnic Displays

*This page contains faint, lightly sketched illustrations—perhaps preliminary drawings or bleed-through from an adjacent leaf—depicting various <term>pyrotechnic</term> <note>From the Greek 'pyr' (fire) and 'tekhne' (art); the craft of creating fireworks for both spectacle and symbolic representation.</note> devices or celestial phenomena.*

At the top of the page is a small, circular cluster of dots, resembling a star or a small firework burst. Below it, there is a larger, denser cluster of circular points.

Centered on the page is a large, radiating burst with numerous long, spike-like rays extending outwards in all directions. This is characteristic of a **"sun"** or **"glory"** firework display <note>These "suns" or "glories" were common in 17th and 18th-century fireworks displays, often mounted on poles or frames to rotate and throw off sparks. In alchemical contexts, they often symbolized the "fixed" sun or the completion of a stage in the Great Work.</note>.

At the very bottom, a faint horizontal rectangular area suggests a ground line or a pedestal upon which the display is mounted.

<summary>This page features faint sketches of pyrotechnic devices, including a large sunburst firework, suggesting the manuscript contains technical or symbolic instructions for displays of light and fire.</summary>
<keywords>pyrotechnics, fireworks, sunburst, technical illustration, glory, alchemical symbolism</keywords>

<header>DIVINE WISDOM</header> <note>original: "THEOSOPHIA"</note>

# **The Human Being**

->Throughout according to all leading, ruling Three Principles<-

*This page consists of a hand-colored engraving of a human figure, serving as a map of "mystical anatomy." The body is treated as a microcosm—a small universe—containing the different realms of existence, from the highest divine light to the lowest dark nature.*

**The Spiritual Centers of the Human Being:**

*   **The Forehead:** <gloss>Holy Spirit</gloss> <note>original: "H. GEIST". Representing the divine breath or inspiration entering the mind.</note>
*   **The Temples:** <gloss>Under-standing</gloss> <note>original: "Verstand". Positioned at the seat of the intellect.</note>
*   **The Throat:** <gloss>Sophia, Mirror of the Godhead</gloss> <note>original: "SOPHIA Spiegel der Gottheit". In Christian Theosophy, Sophia represents Divine Wisdom, the feminine aspect or "mirror" through which God contemplates Himself.</note>
*   **The Heart:** <gloss>JESUS</gloss> <note>The central, radiant golden sun of the figure. In this system, Christ represents the "Second Principle"—the light and love that occupies the center of the human soul.</note>
*   **The Solar Plexus:** <gloss>JEHOVAH</gloss> <note>original: "JEHOVA". Often associated with the "First Principle" of fire, power, and the Father, which must be tempered by the light of the heart.</note>
*   **The Lower Torso:** 
    *   **The Dark Circle:** <gloss>DARK WORLD</gloss> <note>original: "FINSTERE WELT". This represents the "First Principle" in its raw, unregenerated state—the "fire-root" or the abyss of nature before it meets the light.</note>
    *   **The Border of the Circle:** <gloss>Center of Nature: The Matrix: The soul's root of a threefold birth</gloss> <note>original: "Centrum Naturæ: Die Matrix: Der Seelen wurzel dreyerley Gebuhrtt". This refers to the core theosophical doctrine of Jacob Böhme, where the soul is born out of three realms: the dark fire-world, the light love-world, and the external physical world.</note>

*The figure stands upon a patch of green earth, while his legs are shaded in dark, terrestrial tones, symbolizing the "Third Principle"—the physical world of matter in which the human being is grounded.*

<summary>This page presents a central symbolic diagram of the human being as a microcosm, illustrating how divine forces and the "Three Principles" of existence are mapped onto the human body and soul.</summary>
<keywords>Theosophy, Jacob Böhme, Microcosm, Sophia, Three Principles, spiritual anatomy, Jesus, Jehovah, Dark World, Matrix</keywords>

# Preface.

->I.<-

1. The beloved reader receives here, against all expectation, that which he has hoped for over many years. These are the illustrations of the <term>Inner Man</term> <note>The "Inner Man" is a central concept in Christian mysticism, referring to the spiritual or divine soul hidden within the physical body.</note>, which our author kept hidden with him for 15 years until the end of his life, and which we have kept for another 10 years after his passing.

2. They would have continued to be kept secret, had not Love moved again in the Spirit to please its devotees, finally giving permission and strengthening us to entrust them to the press. We have added them to this general new printing of this blessed and expanded third edition of the <term>Anointed Letters</term> <note>original: "gesalbten Briefe"; these are the collected letters of the mystic Johann Georg Gichtel, often titled *Theosophia Practica*.</note> as a lovely ornament; serving at the same time as a key for the reader, within the spirit of understanding, to better comprehend what he reads.

3. We rejoice with you over God’s great kindness, with childlike hearts that wish each other well and praise God together for it.

4. Receive then this gift, which Love has provided even though the printing was already very far advanced, with a humble heart as if from God; and use it, as you do all the writings of this author, for the benefit and salvation of your soul.

5. The dear reader will also, to his pleasant

<summary>This preface introduces the long-awaited publication of mystical illustrations ("Figures of the Inner Man") that had been kept secret for 25 years. They are included in this third edition of the author's collected letters to serve as a spiritual guide for the reader.</summary>

# Preface.

5. will take pleasure in the fact that we have provided him a short introduction to the <term>Figures</term> <note>These "Figures" are the mystical diagrams or illustrations mentioned on the previous page, intended to visualize the spiritual state of the soul.</note>, which we have incorporated into this writing at every appropriate place: We were moved to do this, and our <term>minds</term> <note>original: "Gemüthe"; refers to the seat of emotions and spiritual disposition.</note> were set upon it, as God’s Spirit opened itself and gave us the word to fulfill this desire:

6. Also in order to fulfill, in some measure, that which did not wish to flow from the author’s own pen: Although one cannot express the <term>Divine Riches</term> <note>original: "Göttlichen Reichthum"</note> all at once, but only as much as the Holy Spirit, who holds the key to them, gives one to express at any given time.

7. In that same <term>Outflow of Grace</term> <note>original: "Gnaden-Ausfluß"</note>, we then also followed the <term>Process of Jesus</term> <note>In this mystical tradition, the "Process of Jesus" refers to the internal spiritual journey where the believer experiences the stages of Christ's life, from birth to crucifixion and resurrection, within their own soul.</note>, regarding what our dearest Savior has done in our humanity since then, and has further promoted in the spirit up to this time, in the power of Jesus, who is the author and finisher of our faith.

8. Praise God! That we have lived to see the day for which the author so longingly desired in his own time. The <term>birth-labor</term> <note>original: "Geburts-Arbeit"; a term for the intense, often painful spiritual struggle required for the "new birth" or spiritual transformation.</note> with the outer spirit (which heavily weighed upon the <term>outer man</term> <note>The physical, worldly self as opposed to the spiritual "inner man."</note>) surpassed the natural strength of old age; therefore the <term>Virgin</term> <note>Likely referring to Sophia, or Divine Wisdom, a central figure in Gichtelian and Boehmist theosophy who acts as the spiritual bride of the soul.</note> called her Bridegroom to herself, and imposed this strict and hard labor upon those of a younger age.

9. In which we also had Jesus with us, who allowed us to enjoy the labor of the younger years of our <term>Combatant</term> <note>original: "Streiters"; refers to the author as a spiritual warrior or ascetic.</note> for the common benefit of all fellow combatants. To God
->be the praise, Amen.<-

# Preface.

## To the God- and Wisdom-loving Reader.

->I.<-

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'W' featuring elaborate foliate and scroll-work patterns.</image-desc>
Although I have been very timid and cautious about sharing this <term>little flower of Paradise</term> <note>original: "Paradis-Blümlein"; a common metaphor in this tradition for a delicate spiritual insight or revelation blooming within the soul.</note>—which was opened to me during my great and earnest struggle—with this present coarse and swinish world, which only tramples the noble pearl of the <term>knowledge of God</term> <note>original: "Erkentniß GOttes"</note> and of self underfoot and tears its lovers to pieces; nevertheless, my dear fellow-combatants have overcome me with their repeated encouragement. I have agreed to bring this to light as a guide for those who search for the <term>Mystery of God</term> <note>original: "Mysterio GOttes"; refers to the hidden spiritual reality of the divine working within the human soul.</note> in their innermost <term>ground</term> <note>In mystical philosophy, the "ground" (Grund) is the deepest, uncreated part of the soul where the individual meets the Divine.</note>, so that they may have a mirror before their eyes in which to behold themselves.

2. Also as a reminder and warning, because <term>Adam</term> <note>The author uses Adam as the archetype for all humanity, whose "fall" is a psychological and spiritual event repeated within every person.</note> lost the advantage and awakened the cold, fierce darkness in his <term>forms of life</term> <note>original: "Lebens-Gestalten"; these are the fundamental spiritual forces or "source-spirits" that constitute a person's inner nature.</note>, making it active...

<summary>The author explains his initial reluctance to publish his spiritual revelations due to the hostility of the world, but reveals he was persuaded by peers to provide this text as a "mirror" for seekers. He begins to describe the spiritual fall of humanity through the figure of Adam.</summary>

<keywords>Paradise-flower, knowledge of God, Mystery of God, spiritual struggle, Adam, forms of life, inner mirror, mysticism</keywords>

# Preface

...made, and in all his branches and children, a violent struggle of evil against good, of darkness, as well as of wrath and No, against light, love, and Yes, has been awakened. <note>In this mystical tradition, "Yes" and "No" represent the fundamental duality of the universe: the "Yes" is God's outgoing love and light, while the "No" is the necessary, inward-turning friction or "wrath" that allows life to have a distinct form.</note>

3. Therefore, everyone must be on their guard, diligently and earnestly watching and praying, and also carefully maintaining a sharp watch over all the evil, poisonous influences of the hellish and earthly constellations <note>original: "Gestirns"; refers to the belief that both celestial bodies and spiritual "stars" exert a shaping influence on the human soul.</note>, and paying close attention to the "tongue of the scales"—that is, the desire, longing, and <term>Imagination</term> <note>Here, "Imagination" refers to the soul’s power to "image" or form itself into a particular state, effectively "birthing" its own spiritual reality.</note> of their mind—so that the good is not outweighed and their fall and rift become even greater.

4. This is very easily and quickly overlooked when the soul becomes a little overconfident and careless, turning itself incautiously toward earthly things, and allowing itself to be impregnated by the spirit of this world with an earthly lust, or entertaining a fierce thought against its brother, and rising up in pride <note>original: "Hoffart"</note> above the thrones.

5. For all three <term>principles</term> <note>original: "Principia"; the three fundamental realms of existence: 1. The dark fire-world of the Father's wrath/will; 2. The light-world of the Son's love; 3. The outer, visible world.</note> hunger for the soul: each one seeks the supreme rule and struggles for it until it has conquered, or is itself conquered and bound by the Strongest—namely, the Light, which alone is almighty—and...

6. 8. And what might it profit a man that he loves his earthly life and seeks to preserve it? It is fragile after all, and will finally be lost according to Christ’s saying <note>original: "Christi Ausspruch"; referring to Matthew 16:25, "For whoever wants to save their life will lose it..."</note>. It is much better that one does not put his hand to the <term>plow</term> <note>An allusion to Luke 9:62: "No one who puts a hand to the plow and looks back is fit for service in the kingdom of God."</note> and waits patiently in his simplicity for the Lord’s call, or if one is indeed in the

# Preface

...mind <note>original: "Gemüth"; a central term in German mysticism referring to the seat of the soul's emotions, will, and consciousness.</note> driven by **God's Spirit** walks in **humility**, often calculating the account of its internal powers, [to see] whether one dares to stand with ten against twenty thousand <note>A reference to the parable in Luke 14:31 about a king weighing the cost of war; here used as a metaphor for the soul assessing its strength against spiritual temptation.</note>, and would rather walk slowly than "fly" <note>referring to spiritual pride or rushing into advanced mystical states without preparation</note>, despise his enemy, and finally be overcome.

9. I write this as a warning to those who indeed joyfully enter into the **Lord’s discipleship**; but as yet know nothing of any earnest struggle: For the beginning is commonly sweet, joyful, and very pleasant; but when it comes to the earnest work—that the **soul** must withdraw its will from the **outer stars** <note>original: "äussern Gestirne"; in the mystical tradition of Jakob Böhme, this refers to the influence of the physical cosmos and the "World-Spirit" on the human mind.</note> and turn itself into its **Center** <note>original: "Centrum"; the innermost point of the soul where the divine presence dwells.</note> in **God**, leaving all visible things behind, and must penetrate through the **eighth form of fire** <note>original: "achte Gestalt des Feuers"; a technical term in Böhmean theosophy. It represents a "spiritual fire" or a point of transformation where the soul passes from the darkness of self-will into the light of God.</note>—then it costs labor and bloody sweat, as the soul must wrestle with **God** and men.

10. If you should now love your life and want to look back, then the **Devil** comes with seven more evil spirits <note>A reference to Matthew 12:45.</note> and bars your poor **soul** in all its forms, so that you remain for the whole time of your life in great misery, poverty, hunger, and the earthly **belly-worry** <note>original: "Bauch-Sorge"; an archaic term for the obsessive anxiety regarding one's physical survival and material needs.</note>, a **slave** of the **World-Spirit**, and the **Devil’s** prison-

<summary>The author warns that while the start of a spiritual journey is often pleasant, the "earnest struggle" requires a painful detachment from the material world and the influence of the stars. He cautions that turning back leads to spiritual enslavement and deeper misery.</summary>

<keywords>humility, spiritual struggle, discipleship, Center of the soul, eighth form of fire, World-Spirit, belly-worry, spiritual warfare</keywords>

11. In this time, you are your own maker: If you remain in **humility**, and make an **angel** of yourself, then you are an angel; but if you make a proud, flying **devil** of yourself, then you are a devil, and you cannot blame **God**.

12. The fire of the eighth form is the **dividing-point** <note>original: "Scheide-Ziel"; a boundary or destination where the soul is tested and separated from its earthly attachments.</note>: I have indeed borrowed this figure from **Böhme’s** writing *Of the Threefold Life* <note>Referring to Jakob Böhme's *The Threefold Life of Man* (1620), a foundational text of Christian theosophy exploring the relationship between God, the soul, and the world.</note>, but for the sake of simplicity I have put it into such diagrams, because man has become so very earthly and externalized that he is always speculating about things above himself, seeking that which is very near to him within the **Center of the soul** far beyond the starry **firmament** in high eternity.

13. Also, in the following diagrams, it is painted before your eyes how the **three worlds** <note>In this mystical tradition, the "three worlds" usually refer to the world of God's anger (darkness/fire), the world of God's love (light), and the visible, material world.</note> are within one another, or stand under one another, as the kind **God** has revealed them to me—yes, even led my spirit into all their **Centers**. For I only point out the Centers: If someone wished to make large circles or globes and draw them into one another, they may do so; as I [have done] in the

[it in my] spirit, so have I also recorded it.

14. And although not everyone attains to such direct vision <note>original: "Schaulichkeit"; referring to a state of mystical contemplation or "seeing" spiritual truths directly</note>—for it depends on Divine grace and steadfast faithfulness, victory and overcoming—nevertheless, an earnest warrior will surely become aware of these things within himself through his own feeling and "taste" <note>referring to spiritual sensation or internal evidence</note>, if he walks intimately with God.

15. In addition, in the fifth chapter, I have included and discussed the battle between Michael and the Dragon <note>A reference to Revelation 12:7, interpreted here as the internal struggle between divine and worldly forces</note>, what it is, and how it originates <note>original: "urstände"</note> in the creature through the separation and division of the **One Will** into many opposing wills; also, which temptations befall the **Virgin-Son** <note>a mystical term for the Christ-like spirit born within the soul</note> before he is fully sealed in the **spiritual marriage** <note>the mystical union of the soul with the Divine</note>.

16. And lastly, in the sixth chapter, I have mentioned something of **Prayer** and its **Mystery** <note>original: "Mysterio"</note>—namely, what it is to pray rightly in spirit and truth, as I have learned it from my own experience—because it is an indispensable sword for a spiritual warrior.

17. May the dear reader receive this in love, use it for his own benefit, and thank God, from whom I have received it. To His precious love I commend the reader, and I ask for your Christian prayer for myself.

<summary>The author concludes the preface by outlining the final chapters on spiritual warfare and prayer, emphasizing that spiritual truths must be experienced internally rather than just understood intellectually.</summary>

# Chapter One.
## Preliminary Report.

->I.<-

God-seeking reader! Here, the <term>Wheel of Birth<note>A central concept in Böhmean theosophy describing the eternal movement and "churning" of the divine properties as they bring forth life and consciousness.</note></term> is shared with you according to all <term>three Principles<note>The three realms of existence: the dark fire-world (wrath), the light-world (love), and the outer astral-elementary world.</note></term>, undivided and in its entirety, so that you may see in advance how the forms or powers of the <term>new man<note>The spiritual self that is "born again" through inner transformation.</note></term> are born in their proper order within the <term>soul<note>original: "Gemüthe"; refers to the seat of the mind, emotions, and spiritual will.</note></term>.

2. Form this vividly in your soul, and you will learn to understand our author in the following figures all the more easily, since true understanding resides inwardly within the soul itself.

3. Especially since he wishes to show you here, as an entrance, only generally what the first-created image of God was before the Fall—which remains hidden in the spirit, recognized by the author in <term>practice<note>original: "Praxi"; referring to lived spiritual experience rather than mere theory.</note></term>, and unfolded for you in the figures of the perfect man through the new birth in Christ, <note>A printed curly brace appears to the right of this paragraph.</note>

4. And in you, through the <term>rebirth<note>The process of returning to the original divine state intended for humanity.</note></term>, it will also gradually come into being and be recognized, if you follow the writer in earnest.

## Concerning the Great Mystery of Divine Revelation, according to Time and Eternity in the Human Image.

->1.<-

<margin>#</margin>
<image-desc>Large ornamental drop cap letter 'W' featuring elaborate scrollwork and floral motifs.</image-desc>
When we wish to behold and contemplate man in his deep inner birth, we must only turn with our soul out of the earthly, astral <note>original: "gestirnten"; referring to the influence of the stars or the "sidereal" spirit upon the human psyche.</note>, and elementary life into the inner divine life of Jesus Christ within us. We must call upon this precious Physician for grace, that he might graciously reopen the eyes of us who are born blind by nature—eyes which the devil closed in Paradise—so that we may regain our lost <term>Eye of Light<note>A spiritual organ of perception capable of seeing divine truth, contrasted with the physical or rational eyes.</note></term> and may recognize and behold God within us. Otherwise, this will surely remain a sealed mystery and will be incomprehensible to our rational <term>Star-Eye<note>original: "Stern-Auge"; the aspect of human reason governed by the "stars" or the natural, worldly order.</note></term>.

2. For what we see externally in man and his conduct is common to all animals, apart from speech and the natural governance of the sensory reason. And when man reaches the end of his time, he goes back into his mother, the earth, just as all animals do.

This refreshed me in no small measure and made me very fiery in my reciprocal love for my dearest Jesus, so that I bound myself to Him with body, soul, and spirit, never more to depart from Him or to turn away in suffering and tribulation, firmly trusting that He will never take His Spirit from me, nor allow me to be torn away from Him.

# Of the Great Mystery

6. Since He has also faithfully done this: to Him alone be all honor, power, strength, kingdom, and glory for all eternity, Amen!

<margin><unclear>circulation</unclear> <note>original Latin: "circulatio"; likely referring to the cyclical movement of the soul or the celestial bodies as they seek their source.</note></margin>
7. Man is, in truth, a threefold <term>constellation<note>original: "Gestirn"; in this mystical context, it refers to the "astral" or "sidereal" forces—the influences of the stars and planets that shape a person's physical and rational nature.</note></term> in both his outer and inner birth, as can be seen from this figure. He lives in a constant striving and hungry desire for his <term>Center<note>original: "Centro"; the divine core or spiritual point of origin within the soul.</note></term>, anxiously rolling and turning around it to reach it and find rest there. And even if he reaches it, he still cannot rest until he returns to his first origin, out of which everything was created and born.

8. The first is the outer constellation—the ruler and guide of the natural man. It drives him toward all kinds of arts, trades, studies, and business; it makes people good or evil, rich or poor, high or low, as is plain to see. <term>Adam<note>The biblical first man, representing fallen humanity tied to the material world.</note></term> fell into this earthly governance during his Fall, and he brought all his children under it as well, so that in this lifetime, we cannot entirely tear ourselves away from it without <term>rebirth<note>original: "Wiedergeburt"; the spiritual process of being "born again" to escape the influence of the fallen material world.</note></term> and a difficult struggle.

9. This life, with its seven forms of outer nature, winds its way into the heart—into the sun—which stands with its root in the fire, from which it draws its brilliance and light. Into these seven forms, the devil, that old serpent, has woven himself and has sealed and bolted the soul, of which one may read in <term>Revelation 5<note>original: "Apoc. 5."; refers to the biblical Book of Revelation, chapter 5, which describes a scroll sealed with seven seals that only the "Lamb" can open.</note></term>.

<summary>The author describes human nature as a "threefold constellation" driven by a restless desire to return to its divine origin. He explains how the "outer stars" govern earthly life and how the soul has been "sealed" by the devil within the seven forms of nature.</summary>

<keywords>threefold constellation, the Center, the Fall of Adam, spiritual rebirth, seven forms of nature, Revelation 5, astral influence</keywords>

### Of Divine Revelation

10. The eighth form is the <term>Fire-World<note>In the mystical system of Jacob Böhme, the Fire-World is the "fourth property" of nature—the point of transition where dark, cold desire is transformed into light and love through a spiritual "flash" or "fire."</note></term>, with its fiery constellation, and it stands in the center of both the great world (the universe) and the small world (the individual human). It is the <term>dividing-line<note>original: "Scheide-Ziel"; a boundary or goal that separates two states of being.</note></term> between the outer and the inner man. However, in the process of <term>rebirth<note>original: "Wiedergeburt"; the spiritual transformation into a new life in Christ.</note></term>, the Light-World is placed in the center; there, Jesus has established Himself, having made the Fire-World within our humanity subject to Him.

11. The root of this Fire-World is the dark world (for the origin of darkness, see <note>Jacob Böhme (1575–1624) was a German mystic whose writings on the nature of God and evil deeply influenced later philosophy and Pietism.</note> Jacob Böhme’s *The Three Principles of Divine Essence*, chapter 21, verse 17), along with the dark constellation which holds the Fire-World captive within itself, together with all devils and damned souls. This is the dividing-line that separates good and evil; it also <term>sweeps the threshing floor<note>A biblical metaphor for judgment, where the "wheat" (the righteous) is separated from the "chaff" (the wicked).</note></term> and allows nothing impure to enter the inner heaven to reach God.

12. In this Fire-World now stands the <term>Cherub<note>A reference to the angelic guardian placed at the entrance of Eden after the Fall of Adam and Eve (Genesis 3:24).</note></term> with his double-edged sword, guarding the entrance to the inner Tree of Life. We must either pass through this in this lifetime, or we must overcome it and experience its sharpness after our time on earth is done. At the end of our lives, Moses stands there with his sharp Law, and the devil reads the <term>register of sins<note>original: "Sünden-Register"; a detailed list of every transgression committed by the soul.</note></term>, as those who have experienced this can testify; they warn all people not to delay their repentance until their final moments.

<margin>Note Well</margin>
13. For no human tongue can express its sharpness. Oh, how blessed are those who allow themselves to be purified in this time and have <term>clothed themselves in Jesus<note>A common Pietist metaphor for spiritual union with Christ, often referencing the "wedding garment" of the soul.</note></term>; over them, the second death shall have no power.

# Of the Great Mystery

But woe to those who have neglected this in this time, for they shall wail and gnash their teeth, and even curse the day of their birth, wishing they had never been born, as seen in Jeremiah 20:14 and <note>original: "Apoc. 6: 16"; The Book of Revelation is often cited as the "Apocalypse" in older German texts.</note> Revelation 6:16.

14. From this Fire-World—or more precisely, between this fire and the <term>Tincture<note>In the mystical philosophy of Jacob Böhme, the "Tincture" is the spiritual life-essence or "quintessence" that brings beauty and light out of the harshness of the elemental fire.</note></term>, as the ninth form—the angels and Adam’s soul were created. Of this, the Scripture speaks in Hebrews 1:7: *He makes his angels spirits, and his ministers flames of fire.* <note>The author translates the Latin Vulgate or German Bible quote here into the mystical context of the Fire-World.</note>

15. The hidden dark world contains within itself the first three <term>forms<note>The "forms" or "properties" are the developmental stages of nature in Böhme’s system. The first three (desire, mobility, and anguish) represent the "dark" root of existence before it reaches the light of the "fire."</note></term> up to the fire; outside of the fire, it is called Hell. This is the severe power of God, which the Devil strove for, and thus turned into Hell for himself, just as Adam did.

16. It should have remained hidden in the fire, especially since Christ has locked it once more, had man not opened it in this world through his sins and abominations, thereby turning himself into a devil.

17. When man has now broken through the <term>Cherub’s sword<note>A reference to the flaming sword of the Cherubim guarding Paradise (Genesis 3:24). Mystically, this represents the "fire" of judgment the soul must pass through to return to a state of grace.</note></term> and has thus overcome both God and man, he encounters the Heavenly <term>Sophia<note>Sophia, or Divine Wisdom, is portrayed as the spiritual bride of the soul. In this tradition, the soul’s salvation is described as a "mystical marriage" to Sophia.</note></term> in the ninth form. She refreshes his soul with unspeakable sweetness and clothes it once again with her heavenly essence. Only then does the soul truly become an angel of God, who dwells in heaven and is able to speak with God.

26. 27. And they now struggle with one another within man, even in the seed, for the supreme mastery; and whichever gains the upper hand exercises the supreme rule in the shaping and forming of the creature in the mother’s womb, as is evident in Cain and Abel, and also in Esau and Jacob.

28. From this, two kinds of people grow forth, good and evil, and not out of a Divine Providence <term>Providence: here specifically referring to the theological concept of predestination—the idea that God chooses who is saved or damned before birth</term>, as [worldly] reason pretends.

God has indeed breathed into man the free will, with the **seven forms** <term>seven forms: *sieben Gestalten*; the seven fundamental qualities or forces of nature in Böhme’s cosmology</term>, for the birthing-forth of the eternal Trinity and Divine Wisdom, and ordained for him reason or understanding with the five counselors or senses as an external helper, which is called the **mind** <note>original: *Gemüth*; refers to the seat of emotions, will, and disposition</note>, so that the free will should rule in all creatures and manifest the wonders of God.

68. But only to this end: that he should turn inward, and be subject and obedient to the Maker, and stand resigned in humility before the Divine **ground of light**, to see what God wished to work and manifest with and through him; he was to eat from the **Word of the Lord** <note>original: *Verbo Domini*</note>, as from Christ’s flesh and blood of the inner man, in faith, and not from the earthly fruit, as from the external fleshly body from the **bag of worms** <note>original: *Madensack*; a stark Baroque term for the mortal, decaying physical body</note>.

<summary>This page warns that delaying spiritual rebirth leads to a much harsher purification after death and explains that the human mind and senses were created to manifest God's wonders through humble obedience.</summary>

### Chapter II. Of the Natural [Things]

<margin>Dragon-Will</margin>
69. Thus this free will of God <note>In Böhme’s view, the human will originates in God but has the capacity to turn away from Him.</note> has broken itself off within you from the internal **ground of light**, made itself into its own god, and now rules over reason and the senses with its forms of external nature; it rides upon the soul, resists and contradicts God in the innermost ground, does what it wants and what is pleasant to the flesh, and is a pure devil, a fiery dragon and serpent, which God must now combat, tame and bend, crush and cast out with His strong **wrath-power** <note>original: *Zorn-Macht*; the harsh, judging aspect of God’s power that reacts to sin</note>.

70. Even if God, through His Holy Spirit, already wishes to come to the aid of the poor imprisoned soul from within, and kindle His Divine **love-fire** in it, so that it might come to a bright radiance and shine, whereby the self-will could behold its own hideous dragon-form;

<margin>NB <br/> Moistens</margin>
71. Yet this self-will, through its false desire of the flesh for lust, unchastity, honor, wealth, high-living, and foolish joy, introduces so much **dampness** <note>original: *Nässe*; a metaphor for worldly distractions and material attachments that quench spiritual desire</note> into the **soul-fire**, that it is smothered and cannot come to a burning in God’s love.

->Humans,<-

73. Not all members of the body are a single member having the same function, but rather many, each of which performs its own task for the body; just as in a house there are many kinds of wood and beams, where one serves this burden and another serves that one.

74. It thus hardens itself within itself, and shuts the door of the mind to the Divine voice, so that it might not be heard within its fleshly peace and governance.

<margin>NB</margin>
75. Who now wishes to punish and convict this beautiful self-god? For it is hidden within a tame beast, beneath a human mask <note>original: *Larve*; in 17th-century German, this term refers to a mask or a deceptive outward appearance, suggesting the "self-god" is a monster hiding in plain sight</note>; it is outwardly pious, honorable, and religious, goes to church and to the Lord’s Supper, contributes to the support of teachers and the poor, boasts of Christ's baptism and of being destined for salvation, or hopes to be saved through grace.

76. He knows how to speak from the Scripture as well as his teachers; indeed, he may go so far as to leave the external church, condemn its decay, and make acquaintance with upright, earnest disciples of Christ, investigating their hidden life and language; he even practices outward circumcision <note>Used here metaphorically to describe rigorous self-denial or asceticism</note>, and abstains from his wife, or does not marry at all, and also speaks of **Sophia's** <note>original: *Sophia*; Divine Wisdom, personified in mystical tradition as the soul’s celestial bride whom it must marry to find God</note> chaste marriage bed and her wedding with the faithful soul.

### Chapter II. On the Natural State
<meta>catchword: 82. The</meta>

...speaks like a lamb, and yet is only an **ape of Divine Wisdom** <note>original: *Affe Göttlicher Weisheit*; a common mystical term for a deceptive parody or mocker of holy things</note>, who mocks God, His children, and every upright being.

78. He takes the sign of Jesus upon his forehead, yet is only a hypocrite and a "speaking mouth"—not a true **Deed-Christian** <note>original: *That-Christ*; someone who practices faith through action rather than just words</note> who carries a humble, surrendered will in his actions, placing himself essentially within **Christ’s Process** <note>original: *Christi Proceß*; the spiritual path of transformation through suffering and rebirth, imitating the life of Christ</note>, and who heartily loves the **poverty of Christ** <note>The spiritual state of having no attachments to worldly status or possessions</note>—which is the sole hallmark and touchstone of true Christians—yes, loving it more than all the **treasures of Egypt** <note>original: *Schätze Aegypti*; a biblical reference to Moses choosing to suffer with his people rather than enjoy the riches of the Pharaoh</note>.

79. I do indeed feel the devil’s opposition within my creaturely selfhood, because he desires to remain hidden within man and not be uncovered; <margin>//</margin> but I let Him rule who opens, and whom no one can shut.

80. For self-will loves itself so much, and makes a person so stone-blind, that he can no longer recognize God’s love, nor distinguish it from self-love; he believes that if one merely touches his self-will with a single word, that one is poking God in the "apple of His eye." He would gladly, if he only could, bring fire down from heaven to consume this enemy of his.

81. But your self-will is not God’s prophet **Elijah**, who was subject to the Spirit of God; rather, it is only a self-will of the devil, which has seated itself in your **life-light** <note>original: *Lebens-Licht*; the inner spiritual illumination or soul-fire that governs a person's existence</note> as if in God’s temple, and rules over everything that is of God, believing itself to be God.

<summary>This page contrasts the "Deed-Christian," who follows Christ’s path of humility and poverty, with the hypocritical "self-will" that mimics religious language while remaining prideful. It warns that this self-love blinds the individual, making them mistake their own ego for the voice of God.</summary>

<keywords>Deed-Christian, Christ’s Process, poverty of Christ, self-will, life-light, Elijah, spiritual blindness, hypocrisy</keywords>

# People.

82. **Blind Reason** <note>original: *blinde Vernunft*; a term used in mystical theology to describe human logic that is not enlightened by the Holy Spirit</note> wants to speak on behalf of its own **self-will** and says: "If all people were to walk in **Christ’s footsteps** and follow Him in like-form, the world could not survive, because all industry and sustenance would come to a standstill."

83. But listen, dear Reason: Six hundred thousand men, not counting women and children, went out of **Egypt**; nonetheless, their sustenance did not come to a standstill. The first Christians sold their fields and held all things in common, and yet **Jerusalem** continued to exist.

<margin>Note Well</margin>
84. Self-will is told that it should **possess as though it did not possess** <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 7:30, advising believers to remain spiritually detached from worldly goods</note>; and where God grants him **wealth**, he should not lock it away in his chests and say, "This is my property and that of my children; I will live with it and do as I please and understand, and leave such riches to my children."

<margin>//</margin>
85. Rather, he should say:
> Lord, it is Your property and that of Your poor children; I offer it back to You.
And he should grant the **breadcrumbs** <note>original: *Brodsämlein*; an allusion to the "crumbs from the master’s table" in Matthew 15:27</note> beneath his table to the poor members of Christ, and let his little streams flow out toward them. In this way, the poor person could persevere unhindered and earnestly in his **divine service** and prayers, and bless you, his generous giver, in return.

86. And the teaching and life of Christ and His children is in no way the cause for the rich to become impoverished, or for the **artisans** and industry to stand still.

<summary>This page refutes the worldly argument that following Christ's path of spiritual poverty would collapse society or the economy. It uses the Exodus and the early Jerusalem church as evidence that God provides for those who hold their wealth as a stewardship for the poor rather than as private property.</summary>

87. ...[perish], and the world goes to ruin; but you, blind **teacher of reason** <note>original: *Vernunfts-Lehrer*; likely referring to theologians or philosophers who rely on logic rather than spiritual enlightenment</note>, are the sole cause and the destruction itself, in that you twist the clear letter of the **teaching of Christ** to strengthen the **self-will** <note>original: *eigenen Willens*; a central concept in this era's mysticism referring to the ego that acts independently of God</note> which is already far too agreeable to itself;

88. And you teach that one can indeed serve two masters, worry and gather treasures after which thieves dig, and also hold feasts and compel the rich; you say one should give nothing to the poor, so that he is not strengthened in his idleness and fantasy; and you stop up the dug **wells of Abraham** <note>original: *Brünnlein Abrahams*; a biblical reference to Genesis 26, used here metaphorically to describe blocking the flow of spiritual life and charity</note>.

89. Whereby the **self-will** is stiffened in its own self-interest, draws all the fatness of the earth to itself, and fattens its own, despises the poor, and spits upon them as **idlers** <note>original: *Müssiggänger*</note>.

90. This hinders the poor members of Christ in their course and struggle against **God’s wrath**, so that they must cry out to God for help and rescue.

91. ### Chapter II. Concerning the Natural

[95. ...that] reason <term>Reason (Vernunft): in this context, it refers to worldly logic or human intellect that lacks spiritual insight</term> is blind, and does not know Christ's poverty, nor does it know how to distinguish it from worldly poverty: For Christ’s poor are faithful Christians, who are poor solely out of love for their head **Christ**, and value such poverty more highly than all the wealth of the world.

96. And even if you were to offer all your **Mammon** <term>Mammon: a biblical term for wealth or material property, often personified as a false god or a corrupting influence</term> to a faithful Christian, he would not accept it, because he lives in a total renunciation of all earthly goods and is a truly blessed poor man, yet rich in **GOD**; which a person who is merely poor in the world's eyes <note>original: *Welt-Armer*; someone who lacks money but has not necessarily embraced spiritual poverty</note> will not do.

<margin>[</margin>97. A believer does not make himself poor; rather, the world persecutes him, takes everything from him, and leaves him nothing at all, banishes him from the land, and thus brings him into the right, true poverty of **JESUS**, so that he can neither buy nor sell in the world:

98. And thus, as if forced, yet joyful and willing, he must become Christ’s student and seek **God’s** kingdom in the firm trust that **God** will provide for clothing and daily necessities.

99. In this faith he must also faithfully endure without looking back until his end, or faith will be taken from him, and he must beg and be a **slave** of the world and of the belly <note>original: *Sclave... des Bauchs*; an expression for being controlled by base physical appetites and the struggle for survival</note>.

100. Now if **GOD** did not provide for such His faithful children and household members, then

<meta>catchword: would [be]</meta>

### Chapter II. Concerning the Natural State

[...temple of the] Spirit, in which **GOD** dwells: His work is to pray unceasingly in spirit and truth, both for the land in which he dwells, so that it may go well for it, as well as for all people, that they may be brought to the knowledge of the truth through the outpouring of the **Holy Spirit**.

107. He must stand in the press <note>original: *Presse*; a common mystical metaphor for the "winepress" of shared suffering or spiritual affliction</note> for his fellow brothers, and also help them to struggle, fight, and overcome; and it is impossible for him to serve two masters, or to involve his senses in the business of earning a living; for he is unfit for all outward works.

108. Therefore he must also live constantly sober and moderate lives, and pay diligent attention to the movement of the **Holy Spirit** in his heart, so that he may hear what the **LORD** speaks within him: but an unbelieving "world-poor" person <note>someone who is materially poor but lacks faith</note> concerns himself only with the things of this world.

109. "Where then are such faithful poor Christians?" speaks Reason. "I would very much like to see one." I confess that for the time being they grow very thinly, and those known are easily counted on one's fingers.

110. ## Chapter II. Concerning the Natural State

117. Reason speaks: "Since you speak and boast of such a high Apostolic life, why then do you not also go out into the world to preach the Gospel, but instead sit quietly in corners <note>The German word *Winckeln* (corners) was often used as a derogatory term for the private, unauthorized religious meetings of dissenters, sometimes called "conventicles."</note> and do not put **GOD'S** investment to use?" <note>The original term *wuchern* refers to the Parable of the Talents in Matthew 25, where servants are expected to trade or "usury" their master's money to earn a profit.</note>

118. Yes, dear Reason, you have hit the mark; you would be the first to refuse it if we were to preach discipleship to you and say, "leave your net and follow us."

119. We have no command to go out, but rather to go in; we are called to this, and we must stand still in the will of our Maker, awaiting what He wishes to do or leave undone with and through our will as His instrument.

120. Self-running and rushing with good intentions does not accomplish it, and only works perversely against **GOD’S** will. A still, surrendered will <term>Surrendered will (*gelassener Wille*): A central concept in German mysticism (Gelassenheit), meaning a state of yieldedness or letting go of one’s own desires to be perfectly aligned with God.</term> is more useful and pleasing to **GOD** than a self-acting will of one's own.

121. We desire solely and alone what **GOD** desires, in which we remain; we let Reason judge us as it will, for we know that it is blind in divine matters.

122. Now we know that **GOD** is good, and everything He made was good; therefore, we can in no way blame **GOD** that we became evil, or that we withdrew our divine free will—breathed into us by Him—from the Maker and made it our own.

123. He who is now a pure devil, whom the Spirit of **GOD** in Daniel and John [calls] abom— <note original="greulichen">The text cuts off here at the catchword; it likely refers to the "abomination of desolation" mentioned in the prophetic books of Daniel and Revelation (John).</note>

<meta>catchword: —inable</meta>

<summary>This page defends the practice of inward spiritual "quietness" over outward preaching, arguing that true service to God comes from a surrendered will (Gelassenheit) rather than human ambition. The author asserts that while God created humanity good, evil arises when the human will separates itself from the Creator.</summary>

->Humans.<-

...me like terrible ravening beasts, even loathsome dragons, poisonous serpents, and other worms; it was certainly not done out of hatred, envy, or bitterness:

<margin>NB</margin>
124. Rather, it has been revealed out of a heartfelt love, so that we poor **children of Eve** <note>A traditional term for humanity in its fallen state, inheriting the mortality and sin of the first mother.</note> might one day glimpse and learn to recognize ourselves, and develop a hatred, disgust, and terror of ourselves, so as to turn back like the **prodigal son** <note>A reference to the parable in Luke 15, where a son returns home in repentance after wasting his inheritance.</note> and remember once more our heavenly Father’s house.

125. For even if one rises high in the love of **GOD** and wishes to place all godless damned souls, along with the devils, into heaven—which I will not dispute—this does not depend on **GOD’S** omnipotence, nor on our will; but rather on the will of the damned souls and the devil, as to whether they could ever, in eternity, form a will toward humility and desire to be redeemed from the **Fire of GOD’S Wrath**. <note>In this mystical tradition, "Wrath-Fire" (*Zorn-Feuer*) represents the dark, consuming aspect of God's power when it is not tempered by His light and love.</note>

126. Indeed, I myself would not like to let it come to that, because I believe that what is eternal is no longer subject to any change. I am not writing about those who remain hanging by a thread and must pass through the **magical** <term>Magical (*Magische*): In the context of 17th-century mysticism, "magic" refers to the power of the will or spirit to manifest a reality or "clothe" itself in a specific state of being.</term> Fire of Wrath with unspeakable pain:

127. But rather about those upon whom no **intercession** <note>The act of praying or petitioning on behalf of another.</note> will be able to take hold, and for whom no relief in the fire can be hoped for: these will immediately renounce **GOD**, curse, and flee—

# The Third Chapter.
## Preliminary Report.

### I.

In this accompanying figure, which is the second by our author, it is shown how faith has now come through the forms of outer nature (among which the first three eternal forms of the <term>Center of Nature<note>original Latin: *Centri Naturæ*. In the mystical philosophy of Jacob Boehme, these are the three foundational "qualities" or forces—such as desire, mobility, and anguish—that constitute the basis of all existence, often associated with the "dark world."</note></term> are included as the dark world) into the very center of the heart.

2. There, JESUS rises in the heart, breaks the dark <term>womb of generation<note>original German: *Gebär-Mutter*. This refers to the spiritual "matrix" or origin point where the soul is conceived and reborn into divine light.</note></term>, and is born within us. He takes up his kingdom in our heart and, as the <term>Serpent-crusher<note>original German: *Schlangentreter*. This is a traditional title for the Messiah, referring to the prophecy in Genesis 3:15 that the offspring of the woman would crush the head of the serpent.</note></term> promised by God—whose rising and future presence is from now on endless within us—he continuously crushes the head of Satan, that old serpent, through the holy birth of light. He constantly grinds down the poisonous serpent-beast and casts it into the abyss.

3. Whereupon a new world immediately opens within the will, and the prodigal son—the soul—now returns to the Father and is accepted into God's heart as if into the inner Divine <term>Light-Tincture<note>original German: *Lichts-Tinctur*. In alchemical and theosophical terms, a "tincture" is a spiritual essence or "medicine" that has the power to transform and perfect lower substances; here, it refers to the soul being permeated by the light of Christ.</note></term>. There, JESUS is "put on" like a garment or a body, which is called the "first garment of innocence" (Luke 15:22).

4. Satan envies the soul for this, just as he envied our first parents in Paradise. He constantly [stirs up] God's fire of wrath within the forms...

<meta>catchword: -ten (continuation of "Gestalten," the plural for "forms")</meta>

It uses alchemical and theosophical language, such as the "Light-Tincture," to explain how the soul is transformed and "clothed" in divine innocence.</summary>

# Chapter III. Of the Reborn

[...forces] of nature in the darkness, ignited both inwardly and outwardly; it also tests them forever in the form of fire, which the soul must now firmly resist through faith.

5. This is also a long-lasting struggle, where Love and Wrath sharpen one another, and in which Wrath must reveal Love. This process passes through all ten forms of the inner <term>fire-life<note>In the mystical philosophy of Jacob Boehme, the "fire-life" represents the energetic, driving force of the soul's nature, which can either become a dark consuming fire of wrath or be transformed into the light of divine love.</note></term> until reaching omnipotence. There it becomes manifest that Love alone is God and almighty, while Wrath is merely nature, which must surrender its strength and power to Love. In this way, one essence and being is formed from both.

6. In this state, the entire Godhead rises in the spirit, and God the Father and Son embrace each other within the newborn humanity. Here, the Father hands over humanity to the Son and presses it to His heart, marrying it to the <term>Virgin of His Wisdom<note>original German: *Jungfrauen seiner Weisheit*. This refers to "Sophia," the personification of Divine Wisdom, often depicted in this tradition as the spiritual bride of the soul.</note></term>, JESUS. From this point on, Satan always falls like lightning from heaven to earth and is cast out, and we overcome abundantly in every struggle.

## Of the Reborn Human.

->I.<-

The kind reader should know that I do not write from opinions or books, but from my own vision and experience. I find great difficulty in this task because this person is spiritual and very...

### Chapter III. On the Reborn <note>original: *Vom Wiedergebornen*. This refers to the "regenerate" person, a central concept in Christian mysticism describing one who has undergone spiritual rebirth.</note>

...den light or radiance; it is also as impossible for our external, natural eyes to behold as it is for us to look into the bright-shining sun with natural eyes.

7. And even if one might point to the appearance of Christ after His resurrection as a counter-argument, it must be noted that He was not yet fully glorified at that time.

8. As the story of Paul near Damascus clearly shows us, Paul was blinded by the radiance of Christ's glorified body to such an extent that he had to go without his natural sight for three days. To say nothing of my own experience <note>The author frequently alludes to their own mystical visions as evidence, suggesting they have personally seen this divine light.</note>.

9. Just as the light of the sun relates to the stars, so do our new bodies relate to the body of Christ: He is the sun, we are His stars, one flesh and bone, one essence; the closer and more conformed we are to Christ’s suffering and imitation, the brighter the radiance and light.

10. I can compare the powers of this body to nothing other than the colors of bright-shining gemstones: diamonds, rubies, hyacinths <note>In historical contexts, "hyacinth" often refers to a reddish-orange or blue zircon gemstone rather than the flower.</note>, jasper, etc.

11. These colors, playing through one another, provide such a lovely and magnificent sight that even the angels themselves long to behold it, and the human tongue is unable to express it: For we have only earthly parables, which are but a shadow compared to the Heavenly Being.

...stands, and begins to remember its Father's house.

30. For poverty and need teach one to call and cry out, and to ask God for the strengthening of faith, which the *disciples of Christ* understand best.

## Chapter III. Of the Reborn

59. The first temptation is: When the soul has been cast out from the Egyptian house of bondage <note>A metaphor for the state of sin and worldly attachment, referencing the Israelites' slavery in Egypt.</note> by the spirit of this world and placed into Christ’s poverty <note>A spiritual state of total dependency on God, stripped of worldly ego and material security.</note>, so that she should work in God’s vineyard, place her earthly hunger in God’s promises, and also—together with her faithful helper and Soul-Bridegroom Jesus—through her desire for faith, prayer, and supplication, form, shape, and create her necessities in the inner heaven, so that such things are substantially cast to her <note>A reference to Matthew 6:33: "Seek first the kingdom of God... and all these things shall be added unto you."</note> on earth and sent to her by charitable, pious hearts:

60. If she returns again to earthly reason <note>original: *Vernunft*. Here referring to human intellect when it is separated from divine illumination.</note> toward Egypt, hungering for earthly bread, and then takes up, grasps, and seizes whatever happens to her by chance <note>original: *casualiter*. Meaning by accident or coincidence.</note> as if it were a Divine providence, rather than recognizing it as a devilish temptation; and if she does not pray diligently, standing always in fear of the tempter, and does not keep a sharp watch on Christ’s footsteps within the mind <note>original: *Gemüth*. A term for the inner disposition where the heart and mind meet.</note>.

61. By this, many a brave soul has been deceived, which afterwards lamented and sighed: "Alas, if only I had not trusted! Alas, if only I had kept my loyalty!" though it cannot be changed afterward.

62. For the Heavenly Virgin <note>Divine Wisdom, or Sophia, who in this mystical tradition acts as the spiritual guide and "bride" of the soul.</note> then locks herself into her center, and lets the soul knock and wait outside in vain.

63. And although reason [receives] from the Divine—

65. Therefore, hold onto what you have: to suffer need is a bad guest.

# Humanity

[...and so that the soul does not] fall [into] folly, God fastens an unruly, foolish wife around his neck, so that the great conflagration might be quenched with earthly water, and also that the fiery spirit might have something to play with in his **imagination** <term>In this tradition, "Imagination" (Phantasie) is the creative power of the mind that can either reflect God's wisdom or become entangled in earthly delusions.</term>, and that through such a bond his "flying" <note>This refers to the "high-flying" pride mentioned on the previous page, where the soul tries to soar above its station.</note> might be hindered.

72. 73. But the dear **God** has <gloss>looked upon my simplicity with mercy, and set me free in time</gloss>; for which I remain eternally grateful to Him.

# The Fourth Chapter.
## Preliminary Report.

->I.<-

1. Here follows in the established order the third and fourth figures of the Author, which have been placed at the very front as the head and title of all the writer's works, because they represent the first image of God from the **rebirth** <term>The "Wiedergeburt" or New Birth is a central concept in Christian mysticism, referring to the spiritual transformation where the soul is restored to its original divine state through Christ.</term> in Christ in its perfection, with the **breastplate** <note>original: "Pectorale"</note> or priestly breast-shield of both eternal **principles** <term>In this theosophical system, "Principles" (Principien) are the fundamental layers of reality: usually the dark fire-world (wrath), the light world (love), and the outer physical world.</term> in **Lights** and **Perfections** <note>original: "Urim" and "Thummim," the sacred objects used by the Israelite High Priest to determine God's will.</note>, the light and perfection of the spirit, as well as with the **shoulder-mantle** <note>original: "Humerale"</note> and royal cloak of the kingdom of JESUS over the outer **principle**, concerning which one may read in **Isaiah** 9:6.

2. So that a reader may notice and see right at the beginning where the author aims in all his letters, who, like **Paul**, exerts himself to the utmost to represent every person in that same image, perfectly in JESUS.

3. It is the goal in which the **centers** <note>original: "Centra"</note> in the new human stand in their first Divine order; the first and second **principles** in front, and the third **principle** behind in the **mind** <term>The "Gemüthe" represents the inner seat of the soul, encompassing the will, emotions, and the depth of the human spirit.</term>.

4. All three originate in the heart, and do

20. And it is not unjustly called the **Great Mystery** <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." In Böhmean philosophy, this refers to the primordial "chaos" or the infinite potentiality from which God manifests all things.</note>, wherein two essences and wills are understood: namely the Unity <note>God's singular, peaceful essence.</note> and the differentiated will, which leads itself into a desire, until it manifests as fire and light; where in the fire, the Nature-life is understood, and in the light, the Holy Spirit-life of the Unity is understood.

49. Thus the question is: How could that which God created good have become evil, seeing as the Tree of Trial, <note>original: "Verſuch-Baum"</note> along with the prohibition, had not yet been made manifest?

52. 53. Now we wish to investigate further: from where did the desire for the dividing of the life-forms arise in Adam? We must say that it grew within him out of the principles <note>Foundational spiritual forces or "source-qualities."</note> or life-forms themselves, and in no way out of God, who is eternal love and good, and can will no evil.

In this period, the term carried a neutral or positive meaning of "beloved" or "suitor" in a mystical, romantic context.</note>, and she kisses him inwardly, clothing the soul in her own essence and covering all sin.

### Chapter IV. Of the Inward Man,

...own righteousness, which counts for nothing before **GOD**; by no means: For we are all together conceived and born in sins, and carry about us a mortal body that is full of infirmities.

152. Rather, I look upon the inward man as the true temple of **GOD**, into which one must bring no strange fire <note>A reference to Leviticus 10, where "strange fire" represents unauthorized or impure worship.</note> before **GOD**, but must curse every thought that is not the love of **JESUS** and **GOD'S** fire; because no other fire is in **GOD**, nor will any other be admitted, and also because nothing but love in the light can extinguish and soothe the creaturely wrath <note>In the mystical tradition of Jacob Boehme, "wrath" is a fundamental cosmic force that must be transformed by Divine Light.</note>.

153. The inward man stands with his Heavenly **Sophia** <note>Divine Wisdom, often personified as a spiritual bride or the mirror of God.</note> in a holy and hidden marriage, and must not admit or harbor an unchaste thought, but must immediately struggle against it.

154. How then could an unchaste person stand in the **Holy of Holies** <note>The most sacred inner sanctum of the Temple, here used as a metaphor for the deepest part of the soul.</note> of the inner man? ### Chapter V. Of the Strife of Michael

<margin>NB</margin> <note>original: *Nota Bene* (Note Well)</note> ...and will it is, <note>This sentence continues from the previous page's discussion on how a single thing cannot know itself without contrast.</note> not having expressed itself with its will out of itself from the eternal science <note>original: *Wissenschafft*. 16. Where, however, in the single will there is a distinctness, and also in that which is separated there is a self-will, and thus in a single will unfathomable and innumerable wills arise, just as branches grow out of a tree:

17. So we see and understand that in such distinctness, every separated will introduces itself into its own form, and that the strife of wills is over form—that in the division, one form is not like another, though they all stand in one ground.

18. Just as the evil or counter-will causes the good will to press again toward its origin <note>original: *Urstand*. The primal state or original source from which something arose.</note> as if toward **GOD**, and to become desiring: (For that which is in itself only good, and has no torment <term>torment</term> <note>original: *Qual*. 19. Thus one can also say of the single good will of **GOD**, that He can desire nothing in Himself; since He has nothing in or before Him that could give Him anything.

### Chapter 5: Concerning the Battle of Michael

62. As soon as a person turns their soul around within the body, breaks away from the **light of reason** <term>light of reason</term> <note>original: *Vernunft-Licht*. 63. <margin>NB</margin>
124. It is certainly no small thing to turn from a devil into an angel and a child of God, and to sit with Christ at the right hand of His Majesty, and to be a judge over His enemies.

<margin>}</margin>
125. For this reason, we must also become conformed to the image of Jesus in our life and conduct—in tribulation, persecution, poverty, suffering, hell, and death. This is very difficult for the soft flesh and blood to accept, as it prefers a short, fleeting joy over eternal salvation.

# Chapter VI.

...a <term>magic</term> <note>original: "Magie." In the context of 17th-century mysticism, "magic" refers to the spiritual power of the will to act upon and shape spiritual substance, rather than supernatural sorcery.</note> and desire that grasps what the will wishes to have and ask for.

28. Because the will is very thin, like a thought, and must have a body in which it can work, it takes the promises of **Christ** into its <term>imagination</term> <note>original: "Imagination." Here meaning the creative, formative power of the mind that "clothes" a thought in spiritual form.</note> as its body, and presses inward toward **God** with desire.

29. However, through this desire, the will becomes hard, severe, dark, and anxious, and feels within itself nothing but doubt and "No." This initially troubled me no small amount before I understood the <term>Birth of God</term> <note>original: "Geburt GOttes." A mystical concept referring to the process of Christ being spiritually born or realized within the soul of the believer.</note>.

30. For I did not want "No," but "Yes," and yet found only more anxiety the more strongly I kindled myself in desire, so that I even had to withdraw; but after a little while I returned again, cursed the "No" and the doubt, and took up a desire to penetrate into the light of **God**.

31. There, the Spirit of **God** met me in that pressing desire and, in the <term>conjunction</term> <note>original: "Conjunction." Likely an alchemical metaphor for the union of opposites, here referring to the meeting of the human will and the Divine Spirit.</note>, struck the fire, through which my anxious will felt itself sensibly moved in a lovely joy within a light, and received its answer.

Boehme's system of seven properties or qualities of eternal nature.</note> and the <term>three principles</term> <note>original: "dreyen Principien." These are the three worlds or realms in Boehme's cosmology: the Dark World (Fire), the Light World (Love), and the Outward World (Nature).</note>, of which he writes in all his works, became clear to me.

34. ...one. <note>The syllable "one" (German: "ne") completes the word "a/an" (German: "eine") from the end of the previous page.</note> From this I have seen so much: that his readers—unless they themselves are led into the **practice** <term>practice</term> <note>original Latin: "Praxin." ### Chapter VI.

41. I present to you a **parable** <note>original: "Gleichniß." In this context, a symbolic story used to explain a deeper spiritual truth.</note> of a **seed**: See, when it is sown into its mother <note>the earth</note>, it must die and pass through all **seven forms of nature** <term>seven forms of nature</term> <note>original: "sieben Gestalten der Natur." In the author’s mystical system, these are the seven fundamental properties or qualities—ranging from harsh contraction to light and sound—that make up all existence.</note> before it brings fruit again; no one who loves the truth to any degree can deny this.

42. For the growing power in the seed drives forward through the help of rain and sunshine in the **desire** for growth; for if there were no **magnetic desire** <term>magnetic desire</term> <note>original: "magnetische Begierde." This refers to an attractive spiritual force within the soul that "pulls" toward a particular goal or state.</note> in the seed, the seed would be "deaf" or dead, and nothing could grow.

43. In the same way, the **magical** or **magnetic desire** of the soulful will or **mind** <term>mind</term> <note>original: "Gemüth." A complex German term referring to the seat of emotions, intent, and the depths of the soul.</note> is the creator and bringer-forth of that which the will has grasped in its **imagination**, namely the noble, gentle **light of God**.

44. When you have thus birthed within yourself that beautiful, gentle light—within which the **Holy Trinity** dwells with the **Eternal Wisdom**—then your entire body is light, and you will have great joy within it.

45. But this light does not last long in the outer mind; for the **red dragon** <note>A symbolic reference to the lower, ego-driven, or wrathful nature of the soul that resists spiritual enlightenment.</note>, acting as the "soul-worm" or the **dark principle** <term>principle</term> <note>original Latin: "Principium." Referring to the foundational, dark, and fiery state of the soul before it is transformed by divine light.</note>, hungers fiercely for it. It swallows the light back into its great **magnetic hunger of wrath** <note>original: "Grimmen-Hunger." "Grimm" describes a state of fierce, severe, or "grim" indignation and heat within the soul's nature.</note> in order to extinguish it.

46. If you wish to have your noble light shining within you again, you must simply birth it forth within you once more; so that in your soul a...

# Chapter VI.

...those who still stand outside in the **outer court of the flesh** <note>original: "Vorhofe des Fleisches." Drawing on the layout of the Biblical Tabernacle, the author suggests that most people live only in their physical senses (the outer court) rather than the "Inner Sanctuary" of the spirit.</note>, you should offer prayers, intercessions, praise, thanks, and **atonement sacrifices** <note>original: "Versöhn-Opfer."</note>, and you should present your soul as an **anathema** <term>anathema</term> <note>original: "Anathema." Following the example of the Apostle Paul in Romans 9:3, the author suggests the believer should be willing to be "offered up" or even "accursed" for the sake of saving others.</note> for theirs in Christ Jesus.

54. You must constantly wrap yourself in the love of Jesus and, in prayer, lead this love into the wrath—into its fiery, sharp hunger—so that it may be subdued and quenched.

55. You must not bury your **spiritual pound** <note>original: "geistlich Pfund." A reference to the Parable of the Talents (or Pounds) in Luke 19, where servants are judged on how they invested what was entrusted to them.</note> within your own soul, nor procure heavenly provisions for yourself alone; instead, you must always let your little stream flow out through prayer into all your fellow-branches, and bring your fruits of love and first-fruits to God; in this way, God can bless you and impart ever new strengths.

56. And through such practice, you will progress further into the **Interior** <note>The "Inner Life" or mystical union with God.</note> in a single year than you would otherwise in many years; all who are experienced in this will have to confess the same with me.

<margin>Note Well</margin>

A mystical metaphor: through the believer's prayers, the Holy Spirit "woos" the souls of others to become "Brides of Christ."</note>, who will heartily thank you and God that through your diligent labor, salvation has come to their souls.

A reference to Revelation 12, symbolizing the devil or the fierce, angry nature within man.</note> within them, will not surrender itself to love, and would move against you with animalistic zeal and wrath;

59. Then you must only remain in the love of Jesus, and never let them out of your **imagination** <term>imagination</term> <note>original: "Imagination." In this context, it is not "make-believe" but the focused, creative power of the soul to hold an image or a person in the light of God's presence.</note> and will—

# Appendix.

### A Short and Simple Reflection
->on the<-
# State of Marriage /

How such is to be conducted among heartfelt Christians <note>original: "Gemüthlichen Christen." In this context, it refers to "spiritually-minded" or "sincere" believers whose faith resides in the *Gemüt* (the inner soul and emotions) rather than just outward ritual.</note> with a pure heart and conscience, (with regard to the spiritual marriage of the faithful souls with Christ)

->Wherein<-

Thus, man has cause to be truly ashamed before the animals, for he has become lower than they are. This reflection must drive him to practice moderation in his nature, and to pursue **discipline, chastity, and purity** with all his might, applying himself diligently to these virtues.

# Appendix

render [the] due affection <note>The text likely reads "schuldige Freundschaft," a 17th-century German rendering of the "marital debt" or "due benevolence" mentioned in 1 Corinthians 7:3.</note>; that the wife has no power over her own body, but the husband; likewise the husband has no power over his own body, but the wife; and that one should not deprive the other, unless it be by mutual consent for a time, so that they may have leisure for fasting and prayer, and then come together again, so that Satan does not tempt them because of their unchastity.

10. However, it is far from the truth that the holy Apostle intended, through this, to *legitimize* <note>original: *legitimiren*</note> and *defend* <note>original: *patrociniren*</note> this sinful fleshly lust, lewdness, fornication, unchastity, and impurity in the state of marriage in these modern times: as he has shown his zeal against it in the *above-cited passages* <note>original: *citatis locis*</note>, and many others, indeed in this very *chapter* himself. # On the State of Marriage.

...to dismiss <note>original: *dimittiren*; referring to the act of divorcing or separating from a spouse</note> [one’s wife], although from the beginning it was not so, as the dear Savior himself speaks in Matthew 19:8. Just as Saint Paul himself adds in 1 Corinthians 7:6, that what he mentioned in verses 2, 3, 4, and 5, he says by way of concession <note>original: *Vergunst*; meaning permission or allowance rather than a divine mandate</note>, and not as a command from the Lord.

11. It is therefore far from the truth that this condescension <note>Meeting people where they are in their spiritual development.</note> should be a general rule by which Christian hearts in the state of marriage are bound. The lives of the first Christians in general prove quite the opposite; for one finds with astonishment that as soon as their souls heard the word of Jesus from the mouths of the Apostles, they felt within themselves the power of the **chaste Lamb, JESUS**. The fiery love for **GOD** drove them (which is the best and also the only teacher on this point) so that they soon **spiritually circumcised** <note>A common Pietist metaphor for "cutting away" worldly or carnal desires from the heart.</note> themselves, and the men no longer wished to know women, nor the women men, in the natural sense. From this, great persecution arose against the holy Apostles from the heathens, which they suffered as faithful servants of Jesus, among whom Paul was not the least.

But if the aforementioned word to the Corinthians had applied here, they could have quickly saved themselves from the hands of their enemies by directing the wives back to their husbands and vice versa <note>original: *& vice versa*</note>; but this would have been carnal. If one may speak thus regarding those holy Apostolic men, whose dignity <note>original: *Respect*</note> does not permit such a thought: But it is reported in re-

We even know of examples among honorable, merely natural people who do not commit violence against one another in this matter; a wife might never wish to be touched by her husband, and yet they live well and peacefully together.

# On the State of Marriage

12. The Law <note>The Mosaic Law or the Old Testament legal code</note> clearly instructs us that the marital act <note>original: *eheliche Werck*; a common euphemism for sexual intercourse</note>, even when viewed in its purest form, cannot be lifted up to God, and that it is unclean in God's clear eyes (before whom even the heavens are not found pure). God Himself commanded Moses that he must make a specific kind of linen undergarments for Aaron and his sons to cover the flesh of their nakedness. ## The Bookbinder

Is to paste in the figures in the following manner:
1) The two standing side by side with the heading **PRACTICAL THEOSOPHY** <note>original: "THEOSOPHIA PRACTICA"; a term used in mystical circles to describe the application of divine wisdom to one's life.</note> are to be attached to a small hinge <term>hinge (Fälfchen): a thin strip of paper or fabric used by binders to secure a plate or map into a book so that it can open fully without being lost in the gutter of the spine.</term> before the title page.
2) The Wheel of Birth <note>A central concept in German mysticism, particularly that of Jacob Böhme, representing the seven "source-spirits" or the cycle of spiritual manifestation.</note> is to be placed at page 11 on a small hinge.
3) The remaining 2 copperplates <note>original: "Kupffer"; refers to illustrations printed from engraved copper plates.</note>, however, belong to pages 25 and 63.
**Note well:** The book must not be trimmed <note>original: "beſchnitten"; the process of cutting the page edges to make them even. Excessive trimming was a common grievance as it could remove wide margins or even parts of the text.</note> too closely, because the wide margin should remain everywhere, and it was not for nothing that such large paper was chosen.
